> +{
> +	if (self->pagemap_fd >= 0)
> +		close(self->pagemap_fd);
> +}
> +
> +/*
> + * Allocate a PMD-sized THP, write a pattern, swap it out, read it back,
> + * verify the pattern.
> + */
> +TEST_F(pmd_swap, basic)
> +{
> +	char *mem;
> +
> +	mem = alloc_fill_swap_thp(self->pmd_size, self->pagemap_fd, self->seed);
> +	if (mem == MAP_FAILED)
> +		SKIP(return, "Could not create swapped THP\n");

I think that skipping on failure may hide potential regressions. If the
intent is to skip when you don't get a PMD THP, then you could return
different error codes but do fail the test if swapping fails. Note that
this applies to all the tests calling alloc_fill_swap_thp().

> +
> +	ASSERT_TRUE(verify_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, self->seed));
> +
> +	munmap(mem, self->pmd_size);
> +}
> +
> +/*
> + * Allocate a THP, swap it out, fork, verify both parent and child see
> + * the correct data.
> + */
> +TEST_F(pmd_swap, fork)
> +{
> +	char *mem;
> +	pid_t pid;
> +	int status;
> +
> +	mem = alloc_fill_swap_thp(self->pmd_size, self->pagemap_fd, self->seed);
> +	if (mem == MAP_FAILED)
> +		SKIP(return, "Could not create swapped THP\n");
> +
> +	pid = fork();
> +	ASSERT_GE(pid, 0);
> +
> +	if (pid == 0)
> +		_exit(verify_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, self->seed) ? 0 : 1);
> +
> +	ASSERT_TRUE(verify_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, self->seed));
> +
> +	ASSERT_EQ(waitpid(pid, &status, 0), pid);
> +	ASSERT_TRUE(WIFEXITED(status));
> +	ASSERT_EQ(WEXITSTATUS(status), 0);
> +
> +	munmap(mem, self->pmd_size);
> +}
> +
> +/*
> + * Swap out, fork, then have parent and child write different patterns.
> + * Exercises COW on shared PMD swap entries: writes after fork must
> + * trigger copy-on-write so the parent's data stays isolated from the
> + * child's.  Both processes write and then verify their own pattern so
> + * the parent-side COW path is exercised too (a parent-only-read variant
> + * only proves the swap entry survived fork).
> + */
> +TEST_F(pmd_swap, fork_cow)
> +{
> +	unsigned int parent_seed = self->seed;
> +	unsigned int child_seed = ~self->seed;
> +	unsigned int post_seed = self->seed ^ 0xa5a5a5a5;
> +	char *mem;
> +	pid_t pid;
> +	int status;
> +
> +	mem = alloc_fill_swap_thp(self->pmd_size, self->pagemap_fd, parent_seed);
> +	if (mem == MAP_FAILED)
> +		SKIP(return, "Could not create swapped THP\n");
> +
> +	pid = fork();
> +	ASSERT_GE(pid, 0);
> +
> +	if (pid == 0) {
> +		fill_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, child_seed);
> +		_exit(verify_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, child_seed) ? 0 : 1);
> +	}
> +
> +	ASSERT_EQ(waitpid(pid, &status, 0), pid);
> +
> +	/* Child's writes must not leak back into the parent. */
> +	ASSERT_TRUE(verify_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, parent_seed));
> +	ASSERT_TRUE(WIFEXITED(status));
> +	ASSERT_EQ(WEXITSTATUS(status), 0);
> +
> +	/* Now trigger the parent-side COW and confirm the write sticks. */
> +	fill_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, post_seed);
> +	ASSERT_TRUE(verify_pattern(mem, self->pmd_size, post_seed));
> +
> +	munmap(mem, self->pmd_size);
> +}
> +
> +/*
> + * Swap a THP out and in repeatedly without data corruption.
> + */
> +TEST_F(pmd_swap, cycles)
> +{
> +	const int num_cycles = 5;
> +	char *mem;
> +	int cycle;
> +

View on lore ↗
Signals: requested changes
Ongoing Submitted 2026-07-24 Last comment 2026-08-03 ⏱ No recent activity [PATCH] squashfs: avoid thundering-herd cache wakeups Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch addresses the issue of 'thundering herd' cache wakeups in the squashfs file system, which occurs when multiple tasks are woken up simultaneously due to a single cache entry being freed. The patch introduces a custom wake callback that selectively wakes up waiters based on their requested block, reducing the number of unnecessary wakeups and improving performance. This is achieved by using a per-waiter state and ordering lookups and publications against sleeping tasks. As a result, throughput increases by approximately 2x in certain workloads, with significant reductions in CPU usage and scheduler wakeups.

5 reviewer comments — 0 participants
0 participants
Usama Arif (author) Neutral LLM
Author asked if there were any reviews or comments on the patch, indicating a desire to know the status of the review process.

Hi,

Just wanted to check if there are any reviews or comments on this patch?

It is attempting to solvie a real thundering herd problem we see in our fleet.
It also doubles throughput for small file reads and stat for squashfs.

Thanks,
Usama

David Laight ↳ replying to Usama Arif Needs Work LLM
The reviewer pointed out that the patch does not handle the case where a waiter is woken up for capacity, but then finds its block already published and shared, leading to unnecessary wakeups. The reviewer noted that the patch introduces a priority inversion problem, where a woken task cannot be scheduled due to its priority or being pinned/real-time, causing all other threads to be blocked.
Show original comment

But it adds a priority inversion problem.
It the task that is woken cannot be scheduled (either because if its priority
or because it is pinned/real-time and the cpu it would be run on has preemption
disabled, then none of the other threads can run either.

I had terrible trouble trying to get a 'thundering herd' from a

Signals: requested changes, priority inversion, scheduler blocking
Usama Arif (author) ↳ replying to David Laight Needs Work LLM
The author is addressing David Laight's concern about the patch potentially causing priority inversion, which he disputes by explaining that there is no resource owner and no handoff in this case. He also clarifies that the broadcast wakeups are not blocked and only make other waiters runnable so they can re-test cache->unused. The author acknowledges a potential issue with latency inheritance but plans to address it in v2.
Show original comment 
Hi,

Thanks for looking at this.

I don't think "priority inversion" fits: it needs a resource with an
owner (Documentation/locking/rt-mutex-design.rst), and here the woken
task owns nothing.  There is no handoff either - we re-test
cache->unused with no queue-position or owner check, so the freed entry
is not reserved for the wakee.  Any caller takes it immediately,
including one that never slept.  If the wakee is slow to reach a CPU,
only the wakee waits.  Nor is a wakeup to a busy CPU blocked:
try_to_wake_up() enqueues and sets need_resched, with a resched IPI on
SMP, and preempt-disabled sections are bounded.

And one squashfs_cache_put() frees exactly one entry, so at most one
waiter can progress from it however many you wake.  The broadcast just
makes the rest runnable so they can re-test cache->unused, find zero and
sleep again.  Across several hosts in the Meta fleet this is the normal
picture; one example was 19,511,556 wakeups for 1,045,132 releases in
30s, i.e. 18.7 wakeups per release that freed a single entry.

This is the same shape as 0ddad21d3e99 ("pipe: use exclusive waits when
reading or writing"): where the kernel sets wake_next_reader on the
exclusive wakeup, clears it under the pipe mutex if the pipe turned out
empty, and wakes the next reader after unlocking. That has been in since
v5.6 on the pipe read path, with TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE waiters.

Note this isn't blanket wake-one: the publish wake passes
nr_exclusive == 0, so all waiters for that block are released together,
and wake_up_all() on entry->wait_queue is untouched.
squashfs_cache_put() already passed nr_exclusive == 1 under a "wake one
up" comment; the non-exclusive waiters just made it inert.

Fair point though: the handoff is serial and FIFO ignores priority, so a
waiter inherits the latency of the one ahead of it.  I'll say so in v2.
The filebench run is 128 threads on 32 CPUs - 4x oversubscribed, which
is where that should hurt most - and mean latency went down from 11.30 to
5.05 ms/op.

Thanks,
Usama
View on lore ↗
Signals: acknowledges a fix is needed, plans to address an issue in v2
Phillip Lougher ↳ replying to Usama Arif Neutral LLM

[PATCH v2] squashfs: avoid thundering-herd cache wakeups Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch modifies the squashfs cache to avoid 'thundering herd' wakeups, which occur when multiple tasks are woken up simultaneously due to a single event. The patch makes waits exclusive by enqueuing while holding the cache lock, ensuring that a concurrent lookup either sees the waiter queued or the waiter sees the block published. This change reduces CPU usage spikes and improves performance in certain workloads.

No activity

Last comment 2026-08-07 Re: [PATCH v4] mm/hugetlb_cma: Fix null nodemask dereference in hugetlb_cma_alloc_frozen_folio Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch fixes a null pointer dereference in the hugetlb_cma_alloc_frozen_folio function by changing its behavior when encountering a NULL nodemask. Instead of falling back to all N_MEMORY nodes, it will now fall back to the task's hardwall cpuset, as defined by &cpuset_current_mems_allowed. This change ensures that memory allocation policies are respected even in the presence of a NULL nodemask.

Usama Arif (author) ↳ replying to David Laight Neutral LLM
The author is addressing David Laight's concern about the patch being a 'thundering herd' problem, specifically whether it constitutes a priority inversion issue. The author explains that this is not a priority inversion because the woken task owns nothing and there is no handoff or resource contention. They also provide additional context about the squashfs_cache_put() function and its interaction with exclusive waits.
Show original comment 
Hi,

Thanks for looking at this.

I don't think "priority inversion" fits: it needs a resource with an
owner (Documentation/locking/rt-mutex-design.rst), and here the woken
task owns nothing.  There is no handoff either - we re-test
cache->unused with no queue-position or owner check, so the freed entry
is not reserved for the wakee.  Any caller takes it immediately,
including one that never slept.  If the wakee is slow to reach a CPU,
only the wakee waits.  Nor is a wakeup to a busy CPU blocked:
try_to_wake_up() enqueues and sets need_resched, with a resched IPI on
SMP, and preempt-disabled sections are bounded.

And one squashfs_cache_put() frees exactly one entry, so at most one
waiter can progress from it however many you wake.  The broadcast just
makes the rest runnable so they can re-test cache->unused, find zero and
sleep again.  Across several hosts in the Meta fleet this is the normal
picture; one example was 19,511,556 wakeups for 1,045,132 releases in
30s, i.e. 18.7 wakeups per release that freed a single entry.

This is the same shape as 0ddad21d3e99 ("pipe: use exclusive waits when
reading or writing"): where the kernel sets wake_next_reader on the
exclusive wakeup, clears it under the pipe mutex if the pipe turned out
empty, and wakes the next reader after unlocking. That has been in since
v5.6 on the pipe read path, with TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE waiters.

Note this isn't blanket wake-one: the publish wake passes
nr_exclusive == 0, so all waiters for that block are released together,
and wake_up_all() on entry->wait_queue is untouched.
squashfs_cache_put() already passed nr_exclusive == 1 under a "wake one
up" comment; the non-exclusive waiters just made it inert.

Fair point though: the handoff is serial and FIFO ignores priority, so a
waiter inherits the latency of the one ahead of it.  I'll say so in v2.
The filebench run is 128 threads on 32 CPUs - 4x oversubscribed, which
is where that should hurt most - and mean latency went down from 11.30 to
5.05 ms/op.

Thanks,
Usama

Last comment 2026-08-06 Re: [PATCH v4] mm/hugetlb_cma: support percentage-based hugetlb_cma reservation Acked-by Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch adds support for reserving hugepage memory based on a percentage of the total available memory, which is useful in heterogeneous fleets where different nodes have varying amounts of memory. The reserved size is rounded down to a multiple of the architecture's gigantic hugepage size and may become zero if it's not enough to reserve at least one hugepage. This patch addresses a limitation in the current implementation where only fixed-size reservations were possible.
